 
 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
This document provides information about the e-terraplatform Energy 
Management System (EMS) schema modeling strategy and the topology 
building, table building, and validation procedures that make up a 
substantial part of the EMS modeling effort. 
Software Version: e-terra modeler 3.1.3 
Release Date: July 2008

Conventions 
The following conventions are used throughout this document. Commands 
that are particular to an operating system are shown with the 
corresponding prompt symbol. 
Command Prompts 
Operating 
System 
Prompt Description 
UNIX % All commands preceded by a percent sign prompt 
(%) are issued from a UNIX terminal window. 
Note that all UNIX commands are case-sensitive. 
Windows > All commands preceded by a greater than sign 
prompt (>) are issued from the Windows 
command-line window. 
All Operating 
Systems 
 The absence of any prompt character before a 
command indicates that the command is valid on 
all operating systems. 
 
Command Strings 
Operating 
System 
Delimiter Description 
UNIX Italics Text in italics indicates information you must 
supply. (*) 
UNIX [ ] Text enclosed in square brackets “[ ]” indicates 
optional qualifiers, arguments, or data. (*) 
All Operating 
Systems 
Select When used in command strings, the term “Select” 
means placing the pointer over the specified item 
and pressing the left (default) mouse button. 
(*) Note: All UNIX commands are case-sensitive and must be typed exactly as 
shown. 
 
Change Summary 
The following changes were made to this document for this release: 
• Minor editorial and formatting corrections. 
 
 
1. Introduction to EMS Schema Modeling 
This chapter provides information about the rationales that are the 
foundation of EMS schema modeling. 
1.1 Modeling Environment 
One of the major tasks in building EMS applications is data modeling. It 
involves identifying and defining EMS-related entities, along with their 
attributes and relationships. Based on the defined schema, database 
populations can be built for a specific EMS application. 
In the previous e-terraplatform environment, the power system data model 
was built by subsystem-specific modeling applications, such as 
SCADAMDL. Each subsystem modeling application wrote data to its own 
database schema that served the subsystem applications, such as 
SCADA Scanner. These modeling applications also performed schema 
validation upon request. 
While this approach preserved application and subsystem independence, 
it required greater effort coordinating the development of a valid EMS 
operations model. This approach also caused the duplication of data entry 
when entities were shared across two or more subsystem databases. 
The e-terramodeler approach provides a central mechanism for building, 
maintaining, and validating the subsystem models and synchronizing the 
development of the EMS operations model. 
To keep the existing e-terraplatform functionality stable and unchanged, 
all the model data required by the e-terraplatform applications continues 
to reside in the corresponding e-terrahabitat/Hdb databases. The 
e-terramodeler schema does not replace the existing modeling clone 
schemas (Netmodel, Genmodel, SCADAmodel, and DTSmodel). Instead, 
it adds a common model schema that consolidates the commonality of the 
four e-terraplatform subsystem models and establishes the links between 
them. 
This new common model, referred to as “PowerModel”, resides in an 
object-oriented repository. The classes in the PowerModel provide a 
sound foundation for building an object-oriented power system model, and 
they provide for good alignment with the emerging EPRI Common 
Information Model (CIM) specification. 
The common PowerModel schema is accessible and editable by the 
users. Just like the e-terraplatform database definitions, project 
developers and customers can add or modify the common classes as 
needed with the custom software development. e-terramodeler provides a 
Schema Browser tool that provides direct access to the PowerModel 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page 1 Introduction to EMS Schema Modeling 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
 
schema and read-only access to the e-terraplatform subsystem schema 
(refer to the ModelerUI Programmer’s Guide for usage information). 
1.2 Data Entry Guidelines 
The following data entry guidelines apply to fields in ModelerUI general 
edit forms and tables: 
• Text Entries: All text entries in e-terraplatform database fields must 
be in uppercase characters. This requirement exists because, although 
both ModelerUI and e-terrahabitat accept lowercase entries, other 
application programs expect uppercase entries only. 
Note: Fields that identify the unique name of a record must not contain 
embedded spaces or periods. This requirement exists because some 
applications remove spaces when identifying records between 
applications. 
• Free-Form Text Fields (e.g., Description fields or Addendum entries): 
These are not validated, so they accept both upper- and lowercase 
alphabetic characters, as well as embedded spaces and periods. 
• Number of Characters 
− Alpha and Alphanumeric Fields: Accept as many characters as 
the field allows you to enter. The number varies depending on the 
field. 
− Numeric Fields: All numeric entries are automatically rounded to a 
specified number of decimal places when saved. 
• Data Format: An interactive validation error message displays when 
the wrong data format (alphabetic, alphanumeric, or numeric) is 
entered in a field. 
• Field Type 
− Required fields have a yellow background (common classes) or 
result in a validation error message if left blank (Hdb fields). 
− Optional fields are not validated, and may be left blank. 
− Display-only fields appear grayed out, and do not accept user 
entries. 
1.2.1 Record Creation and Maintenance Rules 
If the model builder complies with the work rules listed below, 
e-terramodeler will create and maintain a federated record as expected. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page 2 Introduction to EMS Schema Modeling 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
 
• For record creation, the model bu ilder must enter data in all the 
required fields. 
When the modeler has entered data in all the fields with yellow 
backgrounds, e-terramodeler will create a record in the prescribed 
subsystem database, provided the required parent records exist (see 
the next item). 
• The parent record must already exist in the subsystem database. 
For example, when the model builder defines a generating unit and 
saves its definition, e-terramodeler attempts to capture the definition in 
the PowerModel database, and it creates a corresponding “generating 
unit” record in each subsystem database. If the parent substation 
records exist, e-terramodeler creates a UNIT record in GENMOM, a 
UN record in DTSMOM, a UN record in NETMOM, and a DEVICE 
record in SCADAMOM. 
If the parent records do not exist, the data transfer to the insufficient 
subsystem database(s) will fail, and the associated Data Transfer 
check boxes will remain unchecked. 
Note: If some aspect of record creation or data transfer fails during a 
save, a data transfer bookmark is created. 
• The modeler must sometimes provide additional data to complete 
record federation. 
For example, in order to create a new UNIT record in the GENMOM 
database, the modeler must also identify the unit’s Plant Controller on 
the Generating Unit form. Without specifying the Plant Controller, the 
record creation and data transfer to the GENMOM database will fail, 
and the Generation data transfer option check box will remain 
unchecked. 
• The modeler must use check boxes on Data Transfer tabs to 
propagate federated records to other databases or delete them from 
their own databases. 
e-terramodeler manages the federated EMS model by keeping track of 
all record relationships. If the model builder must propagate an existing 
record to another subsystem database, or delete it, a data transfer 
check box must be toggled and the existing record re-saved. 
1.2.2 Data Transfer Check Box Usage 
Data transfer check boxes reside on the Data Transfer Options tab of 
general edit forms of federated records. These check boxes serve the 
following purposes: 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page 3 Introduction to EMS Schema Modeling 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
 
• To confirm whether data transfe r operations completed successfully 
when a definition was saved. 
• To confirm whether an upload operation achieved its goal of populating 
the target subsystem databases (clones). 
• (For an existing federated record) To specify whether a record 
previously created should be removed from a subsystem database. 
• (For an existing federated record) In special cases, to further 
propagate a record. 
1.2.3 Data Entry Quick Keys 
“Quick keys” are key combinations that perform the same action as a 
dropdown menu option. For example, “CTRL+S” performs the same action 
as the Save option in the Edit menu. 
In addition to shortcuts for dropdown menu options, ModelerUI also 
supports quick keys for functions that are common to most Microsoft 
Windows–based applications, such as CTRL+C (Copy) and CTRL+V 
(Paste). 
Table 1 lists all of the supported quick keys: 
 
Table 1: Quick Keys 
Quick Key Action 
CTRL+A Toggles a button or check box setting ON or OFF. 
CTRL+N Inserts a new record of the same type as the one 
selected. 
CTRL+B Inserts a new record before the one currently displayed. 
CTRL+L Inserts a new record after the one currently displayed. 
CTRL+S Saves the current record. 
CTRL+Z Cancels edits to the current record before it is saved, and 
reverts to the original data. 
CTRL+U “Undo” the last saved record — i.e., cancels edits to the 
last record saved, and reverts to the original data. 
CTRL+X Cuts selected text. 
CTRL+C Copies selected text. 
CTRL+V Pastes copied text.

1.4 EMS Schema Dataset Validation 
Data entered into the e-terramodeler system is validated in two ways: 
• “Interactive validation” occurs w henever a record is saved, before the 
e-terrahabitat databases are updated. 
• “Bulk validation” incorporates changes, adds or deletes component 
data structures, and prepares the revised EMS system operations 
model for online use. 
The e-terramodeler Validation Tool allows you to run a bulk validation 
sequence in one of four modes: minimum, typical, full, or expert. In 
addition, the tool shows the status and date/time of execution, and the 
resulting validation messages. e-terramodeler also provides a print 
function for hard-copy output of messages. 
A bulk validation sequence is run from the Validation Tool window, using 
one of four execution modes: minimum, typical, full, or expert user. After 
bulk validation, records containing invalid or incorrect information (if any) 
will display on the Validation Tool window, followed by an error message. 
For more information about e-terramodeler error messages, select the 
Validation Messages option in the Help menu. 
Bulk validation is a background process. Running it will not prevent other 
users from browsing, but any attempts to save or validate records before a 
bulk validation run has been completed will be blocked by the system, as 
explained in Concurrent Record Access Restrictions below. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page 6 Introduction to EMS Schema Modeling 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
 
1.5 Concurrent Record Access Restrictions 
The ADE Foundation and the supporting database system provide a 
concurrency control mechanism to allow simultaneous data access and 
entry to multiple users. 
In cases when a single record is being updated concurrently by more than 
one user, the modeling system accepts the first record saved and blocks 
all other updates. In addition, users will get a busy condition when the 
system is performing a Save operation, Archive Retrieval, or a bulk 
validation sequence initiated by another user. These changes will then 
need to be resubmitted. 
Data can be browsed during a bulk validation run, although all Saves are 
blocked until the validation process has been completed. Similarly, no 
changes are accepted while the data models are being archived. 
To run bulk validation, enter details in the fields provided, and click the 
Start button on the toolbar of the Validation Tool window. 
 
 
Figure 1: Validation Tool Window 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page 7 Introduction to EMS Schema Modeling 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
 
• Available Validators 
− Validation Sequence: Dropdown list of valid execution mode 
options.

(General), NETMODEL (General), NETMODEL (Map with

∗ Minimum: Includes SCADAMDL (General), GENMODEL 
(General), NETMODEL (General), and Alarm.

− Parameters: Click the Parameters button to display the Validation

1.8 Capturing Session-Specific Validation Messages 
The ModelerUI application provides an interactive method of importing 
and exporting validation output on demand, and a default method of 
exporting session-specific validation messages. These messages are sent 
in ASCII file format to a shared directory on the server machine. Users can 
then import the messages and work on correcting the errors in parallel. 
The Export and Import buttons in the Validation window toolbar provide 
support for the interactive method. The user is prompted for a 
session-specific file name for both the export and import operations. If the 
user has chosen the Automatic Export option using the Validation window 
Parameters dialog box, the root of the file name will contain session 
identification as follows: 
<Host>_<Configuration>_<Context>_<SequenceName>.TXT 
To specify the automatic export of session-specific validation messages, 
complete these steps: 
1. Click the Parameters button in the Validation window banner. 
2. Set the Automatic Export when Validation Sequence Completes check 
box ON. (Default: Automatic Export check box is OFF.) 
3. Click OK. 
 
 
2. Database Mapping 
This chapter provides information about the database mapping 
methodologies that allow other EMS applications to extract data from or 
transmit data to the Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition (SCADA) 
database. 
Shareable data reside in “mappable” records. Prior to onlining the 
subsystem databases into the operational system, subsystem validation 
programs strategically “map” these records. These programs explicitly 
look for records and designate the physical addresses the records will 
have in addressable memory in the real-time environment. 
2.1 Records 
A “record” is a group of records that uniquely identifies a particular 
measurement or device. This consists of ID fields, and it may also include 
other fields that must contain specific information. 
The transfer of measurements between SCADAMOM, NETMOM, and 
GENMOM can take place only if the entire record for the measurement in 
SCADAMOM corresponds to the record for that component in the other 
databases. In other words, the mapping software must be able to identify 
a component in one database as being the same component identified in 
another database, and it does this by matching the records. 
2.2 Record Composition 
The rules for record composition are as follows: 
• ANALOG or POINT (status) records consist of all the parent records 
except the MEAS record. 
• CTRL records consist of all the parent records except the MEAS and 
POINT records. 
• SETPNT mappable records consist of all the parent records except the 
MEAS and ANALOG records. 
2.3 Name Format 
Each measurement in the SCADA database has a unique system name. 
In the e-terramodeler modeling system, the name is a composite of four 
identifiers: 
• Substation 
• Device Type 
• Device 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
13
 
• Data Point: Analog (or) St atus (or) Accumulator 
Each of these names must begin with a letter or number and can contain 
only uppercase alphanumeric characters (0–9, A–Z), $, and _ 
(underscore). Use of the $ character is not recommended. Embedded 
blanks are not allowed. 
The actual field names and sizes are shown below: 
• ID_SUBSTN C*8 
• ID_DEVTYP C*6 
• ID_DEVICE C*14 
• ID_POINT C*4 / ID_ANAL OG C*4 / ID_COUNT C*4 
The first line above shows that the substation identifier is the ID field on 
the SUBSTN record, and it is 8 characters long. 
The concatenation of these four fields produces a unique system identifier 
for any measurement, called the “composite identifier” or “composite ID”. 
Note: The Equipment Type (DEVTYP record IDs) Reference Table 
provides the Map to Device field for the user to enter a custom map name. 
This map name will be used to map SCADA devices of this equipment 
type to components in the network and generation models. 
Similarly, the SCADA Analog Names Reference Table and the SCADA 
Point Names Reference Table provide a Map Name field for the user to 
enter a custom map name. The e-terramodeler modeling system then 
uses this custom name to create the composite keys that map the SCADA 
run-time database to the SCADA ANALOG and POINT records assigned 
to components in the network and generation models. 
2.3.1 Sample Database Structure 
The databases resident in the e-terramodeler modeling system are 
record-oriented and hierarchical. This implies that all of the measurements 
for a particular field device, such as a circuit breaker or a transformer, are 
all modeled under one device. Thus, a typical portion of the SCADA 
database might look like the following: 
Record type  Value of ID Field in Record Instance 
SUBSTN    ID = GREEN 
DEVTYP    ID = CB 
 DEVICE ID = 422_EAST 
  ANALOG ID = MW 
  ANALOG ID = MVAR 
  POINT ID = STAT 
  POINT ID = RECL 
DEVTYP    ID = XFMR 
 DEVICE ID = T1 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
14
 
  POINT ID = TAP 
  ANALOG ID = AMP 
 
The composite IDs for the records in the GREEN substation hierarchy are 
as follows: 
For the Switching Device “CB”, they are: 
GREEN CB 422_EAST MW 
GREEN CB 422_EAST MVAR 
GREEN CB 422_EAST STAT 
GREEN CB 422_EAST RECL 
 
For the Transformer “XFMR”, they are: 
GREEN XFMR T1 TAP 
GREEN XFMR T1 AMP 
 
The SCADA database supports many other attributes or fields on each 
record type, and many additional record types. 
Note: In order for composite names to be unique within the mapping 
function, the individual names must be unique under their “parent” record; 
in other words, there can be only one 422_EAST device under the 
GREEN CB. The name “422_EAST” could be used as a device name 
under a different device type record or under another substation. 
2.3.2 Composite ID Usage 
The composite ID serves these purposes: 
• Serves as the default data packet identifier in communications within 
the e-terramodeler systems. 
• Links data points in the SCADA database with displays. 
• Serves as the data point ident ifier in one-line FG pictures. 
• Appears as the name on the automatically generated tabular displays. 
• Appears in Alarm and Event entries. 
Some portions of the name are used to map between the e-terrascada, 
e-terratransmission, e-terrageneration, and e-terrasimulator databases 
(see the following sections for discussions of mapping). 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
15
 
2.4 Mapping Generation Subsystem Components 
Corresponding mappable records in SCADAMOM and GENMOM do not 
simply have a correspondence of IDs. They have a correspondence of 
some fields on the records, and they require certain fields in SCADAMOM 
records to have specific hard-coded values. 
For example, for mapping to control records (CTRL) for PLCs, the 
SUBSTN ID (SCADAMOM) must equal the PL ID (GENMOM), and the 
DEVTYP ID must have the value “PLC”. 
 
Figure 4 illustrates the SCADAMOM hierarchy for analog and status 
measurement records (ANALOGs and POINTs) and control records 
(CTRLs and SETPNTs). 
 
SUBSTN   (the substation) 
 | 
 +-DEVTYP   (the device type) 
  | 
  +-DEVICE  (the device) 
  | 
  +-MEAS  (the measurement group) 
   |  
   +-ANALOG (the analog measurement) 
   | | 
   | +-SETPNT (the setpoint control) 
   |  
   +-POINT (the status measurement) 
   | | 
   | +-CTRL (the raise/lower control) 
   | 
   +-COUNT (the MWH accumulator measurement) 
 
Figure 4: Hierarchy for SCADAMOM Measurement Records 
2.4.1 Special Measurement Mappings to the Generation Schema 
In GENMOM, the mappable record cannot be defined quite so generically. 
There are many very different mapping needs in GENMOM, so the 
records and fields for mappable records vary in each case. 
The following mapping is supported between SCADAMOM and 
GENMOM: 
• Control records (CTRL, SETPNT) for PLCs. 
• Analog measurement record s for the operating area. 
• Analog measurement re cords for the plants. 
• Analog and status measurem ent records for the PLCs. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
16
 
• Analog and status measurem ent records for the UNITs. 
• Analog measurement re cords for the TIEs. 
• Analog measurement re cords for the TYLNs. 
2.4.2 Mapping e-terratransmission Subsystem Components 
Records in the network model (NETMOM database) are mapped to 
records in the SCADA model (SCADAMOM database). This mapping is 
done offline, normally in the e-terramodeler environment. The mapping 
program should be executed whenever modeling changes are made to 
either database. The result of the mapping is a list of individual record 
maps to SCADA ANALOG records, stored in the MEAS record list 
(NETMOM), and a list of maps to SCADA POINT records, stored in the 
STAT record list. Each map that is documented in the MEAS and STAT 
record lists includes the “key” of the SCADA record that is mapped to. This 
key can later be presented to the SCADA Applications Programming 
Interface (SCAPI), in preparation for transferring data between the two 
models. 
The purpose of the mapping is so that data transfers between the two 
models can take place. There are two types of data transfers between 
these models: 
• In an EMS or e-terrasimulator environment, measurement data is 
transferred from SCADA to the RTNET application upon RTNET 
request, for use as input to RTNET’s state estimation. 
• In an e-terrasimulator environment, simulated measurement data is 
transferred to e-terrascada from the e-terrasimulator power system 
simulator (DTSPSM). 
2.4.3 Validation Table Definitions 
The SCADA Analog Names Reference Table and the SCADA Point 
Names Reference Table mentioned above (see the discussion in Name 
Format) are examples of the tables that SMVERIFY will use to determine 
whether a valid name has been captured for mapping purposes. The 
Equipment Types and other “types specification” tables are also used in 
this manner. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide

2.4.4.1 Miscellaneous 
The Miscellaneous tab is used to configure general parameters that affect 
the SCADA model. 
• Site Information 
− Company: The name or identifier of the company. 
− Site: The name or identifier of the site. 
• SCADA Actions 
− Operator Action Logging Category: The name of the alarm

− Inconsistent Alternates Alarm Category: The name of the

Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
20
 
− PROCMAN Flag to Trigger Network Sequence on Rate-of-
Change Violation: The name of the Process Manager (Procman) 
flag that triggers the network application sequence when an analog

• Limit Replacement 
− Logging Category: The name of the alarm category under which 
to signal errors in limit replacement. 
− Area of Responsibility: The name of the AREA record 
representing the area of responsibility under which to issue limit 
replacement alarms. 
• Setup Statuses 
− Cold Start? When selected, this check box specifies that the 
Scanner must do a cold start. In a cold start, all measurements are 
marked uninitialized, and abnormal and unacknowledged flags are 
reset. 
− Dispatcher Training Simulator: When selected, this check box 
specifies that the database is being constructed for the Dispatcher 
Training Simulator (DTS). 
− Enable Historical Data Recording: When selected, this check box 
enables Scanner Historical Data Recording (HDR) at this site. If this 
check box is not selected, HDR will not run regardless of the HDR 
flags on the POINT, ANALOG, and COUNT records. 
− Operator Must NIS Values Before Entry: When selected, this 
check box specifies that the records be removed from service 
before a new value is manually entered. If this check box is not 
selected, the RUSER interface automatically removes the record 
from service when a manual entry is attempted, if the record is not 
already marked as Not-In-Service (NIS). 
− Count All Status Changes in Point Action Counters: When 
selected, this check box specifies that all state changes 
(commanded, uncommanded, and manually entered) will be 
included in the action counters available on POINT records. If this 
check box is not selected, then the action counter will only be 
incremented for commanded changes. 
− Initialize Manual Points to Their Normal State on Cold Start? 
When selected, this check box specifies that the Scanner must 
reset uninitialized, normally manual, points to their normal state. By 
default, manual points that are not uninitialized are not reset. When 
attempting to locate the normal state for a point, the order in which 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
21
 
the states are checked is 00, 10, 01, and then 11. The point is set 
to the first found normal state. 
Note: Selecting this check box will also cause Dispatcher Training 
Simulator (DTS) Start-Over initialization to perform the same 
processing on normally manual points in the e-terrasimulator 
environment. 
− Reset Failed Equipment on Start of e-terrasimulator: When 
selected, this check box specifies that the database is being 
constructed for e-terrasimulator, resetting failed equipment to a 
good status in SCADA on startup of the Dispatcher Training 
Simulator. 
− Record Analog Limits: When selected, this check box specifies 
that the Scanner will perform historical data recording of the initial 
high and low values of LIMIT records, as well as changes to those 
limits. If the Enable Historical Data Recording field is not selected, 
the Record Analog Limits field is ignored. 
• Validation Option 
− Check Entered Limits: When selected, this check box specifies 
that the Verify process will check limit values. Verify will not stamp 
its approval (VFYMOM time stamp) to the database unless the 
limits are checked. 
2.4.4.2 Data Processing 
The Data Processing tab is used to configure data processing parameters 
that affect the SCADA model. 
• Data Control 
− Scan Heart Rate (secs): The number of seconds between scan 
authorizations sent to the SCADA Telemetry Front Ends (TFEs). 
− Wait Time After Controls to Avoid Rate-of-Change Alarms 
(secs): The number of seconds to wait after a control action before 
allowing rate-of-change limit violations to be alarmed. 
− Log All Limit Violation Transitions: When selected, this check 
box specifies that Scanner will log all the limits passed in a limit 
violation, rather than just the most extreme limit. 
− Alarm Unreasonable/Illegal States: When selected, this check 
box specifies that Scanner will alarm and log when a status point 
enters an illegal state, or when an analog violates reasonability 
limits. If this check box is not selected, the Scanner will still mark 
the data quality flags UNREAS, but will not actually issue an alarm. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
22
 
− Keep Peak Data on Analog: Enables peak data processing on this 
analog point. Captures the maximum and minimum analog values 
received since the last time peak data values were reset, as well as 
the time the maximum and minimum were captured. 
• Database Building 
− Time-Ordered Exception List: When selected, this check box 
specifies maintaining a time-ordered exception linked list in 
chronological and reverse chronological order. 
− Station-Ordered Exception List: When selected, this check box 
specifies maintaining an exception list in order of substation 
topology down to the device level. 
− Database-Ordered Control Exception, Inhibit, and NIS List: 
When selected, this check box specifies maintaining the control 
exception, control inhibit, and control not-in-service linked lists in 
database order. 
− Database-Ordered Communications Exception, Inhibit, and NIS 
List: When selected, this check box specifies maintaining the 
communications exception, communications inhibit, and 
communications not-in-service linked lists in database order. 
− Database-Ordered Topology Exception, Inhibit, and NIS List: 
When selected, this check box specifies maintaining the topology 
exception, topology inhibit, and topology not-in-service linked lists 
in database order. 
• Data Filtering and Logging 
− Alarm Initial SCADA Values: When selected, this check box 
specifies alarming ANALOG and POINT transitions from the 
uninitialized state to an alarmable condition. 
− Enable Alarm Filtering: When selected, this check box enables 
persistent alarm filtering. 
∗ Analog Records: If this feature is enabled, and if the delay field 
is greater than zero, then no alarm will be issued for a limit 
violation on this analog unless a limit violation persists for the 
number of seconds specified in the delay field. 
∗ Point Records: If this feature is enabled, and if the delay is 
greater than zero, then no alarm will be issued for transition to 
an abnormal state unless the status point persists in an 
abnormal state for the number of seconds specified in the delay 
field. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
23
 
∗ Note: If alarm filtering is selected for a Point configured with 
multiple change detection (MCD), only the initial delay event 
messages include the MCD information. The final alarm 
does not include the MCD information. 
− Save Fractional Accumulator Values: When selected, this check 
box specifies that fractional parts of accumulators should not be 
displayed until they add up to a whole number. 
− Include Inhibited Items in Exception Lists: When selected, this 
check box specifies including SCADA points on the exception lists 
when they are in an abnormal state, even if they are alarm 
inhibited. If this check box is not selected, then SCADA points that 
are alarm inhibited will not be linked into the exception lists. 
− Log Message When Alarm Is Delayed: If persistent alarm filtering 
is enabled, selecting this check box issues an event message (to 
the system activity log only, not the alarm list) when the analog first 
goes into limit violation and is placed on the persistent alarm linked 
list. 
2.4.4.3 Communications Equipment 
The Communications Equipment tab provides these fields: 
• Alarm Category: The name of the alarm category used to signal 
problems with e-terrascada communication equipment. 
• Area of Responsibility: The name of the AREA record representing 
the area of responsibility under which to issue communications 
equipment alarms. 
• Issue RTU Availability: A check mark specifies RTU callback 
configuration. RTU alarms will be issued for all RTU enable-disable 
events. 
• Log All Path Switching Between Alternate Communication Lines: 
A check mark specifies that all switches between communication lines 
will be logged. 
• Clear All Alarms on an RTUC When the RTU’s Record Is Removed 
from Service: A check mark specifies that all alarms will be cleared on 
an RTUC whose RTUC record is removed from service. 
2.4.4.4 Communications 
The Communications tab is used to configure communications parameters 
that affect the SCADA model. 
• NETIO Objects 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
24
 
− TFECOMM: The network object name to be used as the source 
identifier when communicating from the host TFECOMM task to the 
SCADA Telemetry Front Ends. 
− CONTROL: The network object name to be used as the source 
identifier when communicating from the host CONTROL task to the 
SCADA Telemetry Front Ends. 
− SCANNER: The network object name to be used as the source 
identifier when communicating from the host SCANNER task to the 
SCADA Telemetry Front Ends. 
• Scan Group Information 
− Rate at Which to Attempt Reinstatement of Failed Scan Groups 
and Communication Channels: The number of seconds between 
issuing exploratory scans to failed addresses and channels. 
− Time Model for Reinstatement Attempts: The date the 
accumulator was last reinstated, in mm/dd/yy format. 
− Time Model for Gathering Communication Statistics: The time 
model that specifies at what time, within each hourly 
communication, statistics will be retrieved from the SCADA 
Telemetry Front Ends. 
− Log Scan Group Failures: When selected, this check box enables 
logging of address reinstatement failures. 
− Not to Periodically Reinstate Failed Channels: When selected, 
this check box disables exploratory scans of failed communication 
channels. 
− Zero Communication Statistics Daily: When selected, this check 
box specifies zeroing out communication error statistics on a daily 
basis, before the data for the first hour (midnight to 1:00 AM) is 
stored in the database. 
• Scan Parameters 
− Number of Scans to Authorize: The number of smart scans that 
the Telemetry Front Ends are authorized to issue at the base rate 
without further communication from the host. 
− Repeating Demand Scan Interval (ms): The number of 
milliseconds between each successive demand scan from a 
repeating demand scan request from the operator. This value must 
be greater than or equal to 1000 milliseconds. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
25
 
− Repeating Demand Scan Count: The number of times to repeat 
the demand scan from a repeating demand scan request from the 
operator. 
− Decrease Scan Group Error Count When Good Scan Received: 
When selected, this check box specifies that, whenever a good 
scan is received for a scan address, the error count will be 
decreased by one, unless the error count is already at zero. 
Otherwise, the error count is reset to zero whenever a good scan is 
received (default). 
• Count of Scans 
− Fail Inactive Telemetry Front Ends: When selected, this check 
box specifies that SCADA should fail TFEs if it fails to receive a 
confirmation to scan authorization requests. 
− Scans Without Receiving Confirmation Before Failing 
Telemetry Front End: Maximum number of scan periods that may 
elapse without receiving a confirmation from a TFE. If this count is 
reached and the Fail Inactive Telemetry Front Ends field is 
selected, SCADA will fail the TFE. 
− Consecutive Rejected Scans Before Failing Scan Group: The 
number of consecutive NAKs of smart scans that may received on 
a communications path before all the addresses on the path are 
failed. This field applies only to systems using Streams CFEs. 
2.4.4.5 Intersite Data 
The Intersite Data tab is used to configure intersite parameters that affect 
the SCADA model. 
• Intersite Information 
− Disable Intersite Data Processing: When selected, this check box 
specifies that intersite data processing is disabled. 
− Enable Permissions Checking: A check mark specifies that the 
InterSite Data (ISD) function will perform permission checks on 
request for site data downloads. 
− Enable Logging of Attempts to Download a Prohibited 
Measurement: A check mark specifies that prohibited-access 
attempts will be logged. 
− Data Alarm Category: The name of the alarm category under 
which to signal intersite path problems, such as the path to a site 
going up or down. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
26
 
− Data Area of Responsibility: The name of the AREA record 
representing the area of responsibility under which to issue intersite 
alarms. 
− Scan Rate (secs): The number of seconds between polls sent to 
other sites. When zero, no polling takes place to any other site. 
Must be greater than or equal to the Scan Heart Rate field in the 
Data Processing. 
− Status Point Integrity Rate (secs): The number of seconds 
between initiations of integrity scans of intersite status point data. 
When zero, no integrity scans occur. 
− Analog Integrity Rate (secs): The number of seconds between 
initiations of integrity scans of intersite pulse accumulator data. 
When zero, no integrity scans occur. 
− Accumulator Integrity Rate (secs): The number of seconds 
between initiations of integrity scans of intersite analog data. When 
zero, no integrity scans occur. 
− Communications Equipment Integrity Rate (minutes): The 
number of minutes between initiations of integrity scans of intersite 
communications data. When zero, no integrity scans occur. 
• Alarm Usage 
− Establishment of Intersite Path: When selected, this check box 
specifies sounding an alarm when an intersite data path is 
established. 
− Loss of Intersite Path: When selected, this check box specifies 
sounding an alarm when an intersite data path is lost. 
• Maximums Allowed 
− Site Download Time (secs): Maximum time, in seconds, before 
failing an intersite path to a remote site because the download has 
not completed. 
− Consecutive Lost Polls: Maximum allowed number of consecutive 
lost polls before failing an intersite path to a remote site. 
2.4.4.6 Topology 
The Topology tab is used to configure topology parameters that affect the 
SCADA model. 
• Processing Information 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide

− Topology Processing Enabled: When selected, this check box 
specifies that the database should be initialized to allow topology

− Substation Topology Processing Only: When selected, this 
check box indicates that topology processing will be confined to 
substations. 
− Substation Voltage Alarm Category: The name of the alarm 
category for voltage alarms on a substation. 
− Substation Voltage Area of Responsibility: The name of the 
AREA record representing the area of responsibility under which to 
issue substation voltage alarms. 
• Topology Processing 
− Minimum Voltage Level for Topology Processing (kV): The 
minimum kV of an analog to be considered for SCADA topology 
energization processing. If this field does not match one of the 
KVNAM entries on the VALTAB,SCADAMDL (KVNAM) display, the 
value is set to zero. 
− Percent Agreement for Live State: The percentage of 
measurements indicating a live state needed before declaring a 
device live. 
− Percent Agreement for Dead State: The percentage of 
measurements indicating a dead state needed before declaring a 
device dead. 
− Delay Processing After Status Change (sec): Number of 
seconds to wait after SCADATOP receives a device status change 
notification from SCANNER before processing the affected 
substations. 
− Rate at Which to Check for Analog Drift (Minutes): The rate, in 
minutes, at which topology checks for analog drift will be 
performed. 
• Alarm Information 
− Alarm Low Substation Voltage: When selected, this check box 
triggers an alarm when a substation low-voltage condition is 
discovered. 
− Alarm Return from Low Substation Voltage: When selected, this 
check box triggers an alarm when a substation low-voltage 
condition has been resolved. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
28
 
− Alarm Device Terminal Open: When selected, this check box 
triggers an alarm when a device has its terminal open. 
− Alarm Device Terminal Grounded: When selected, this check box 
triggers an alarm when a device has its terminal grounded. 
− Alarm Device Terminal Connected: When selected, this check 
box triggers an alarm when a device has its terminal connected. 
− Alarm Energized Device: When selected, this check box triggers 
an alarm when a device becomes energized. 
− Alarm De-energized Device: When selected, this check box 
triggers an alarm when a device loses its energized state. 
− Alarm Ambiguous Device Energization: When selected, this 
check box triggers an alarm when the energization state of a device 
becomes ambiguous. 
− Alarm Unknown Device Energization: When selected, this check 
box triggers an alarm when the energization of a device is not 
known. 
2.4.4.7 Priorities 
The Priorities tab is used to prioritize events that affect the SCADA model. 
Valid priorities range from 1 (lowest) to 16 (highest). 
• Priority Levels 
− Demand Scan: The scan priority associated with demand scan 
requests. 
− Control: The priority associated with normal control requests. 
− Accumulator Read: The priority associated with accumulator read 
requests. 
− Accumulator Freeze: The priority associated with accumulator 
freeze requests. 
− Generating Unit Control: The priority associated with generating 
unit output control requests. 
− Exploratory Scan: The scan priority associated with exploratory 
scans issued to failed ADRSes. 
• User-Definable Priority 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
29
 
− User-Definable Priority 1: The first scan priority for passthrough 
scans that are sent to the SCADA Telemetry Front End from a 
process external to SCADA. 
− User-Definable Priority 2: The second scan priority for 
passthrough scans that are sent to the SCADA Telemetry Front 
End from a process external to SCADA. 
2.4.4.8 Sequence of Events 
The Sequence of Events tab is used to configure event sequences that 
affect the SCADA model. 
• Rates (in Seconds) 
− RTU Time Synchronization: The number of seconds between 
communicating with the RTU for the purpose of synchronizing the 
RTU time. 
− Sequence of Events Integrity Scan: The number of seconds 
between integrity scans of the SOE logs in the RTU. 
− Time Bias Calculation: The number of seconds between RTU 
time bias processing requests. 
− Analog Limit Excursions Integrity Rate: Specify, in seconds, the 
allowable rate of excursion above or below an analog limit before 
the integrity of the data is compromised. 
2.4.4.9 Historical Data Recording 
The Historical Data Recording tab is used to configure historical 
parameters that affect the SCADA model. 
• Data File Information 
− Number of Historical Data Recording Files Filled Before Alarm 
Issued: The allowed number of recording files before a warning 
alarm is issued to the operator. If a zero is entered, no alarm will be 
issued when the optimal number of HDR files is reached. 
− Maximum Number of Historical Data Recording Files: The 
maximum allowed number of concurrent HDR files. 
− Maximum Time the Recording File Is Allowed To Be Filled 
Before Closing the File: As a means of limiting the size and extent 
of each HDR file, data can be recorded into each recording file for a 
maximum timer period. After the maximum delay time period 
entered here (in seconds) has elapsed, the current recording file 
will be closed and a new recording file will be created and opened. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
30
 
• Historical Data Recordings 
− HDR Default State 
∗ Record: When selected, this radio button specifies that Scanner 
HDR should begin automatically at startup, as soon as an empty 
recording file is available. 
∗ Off: When selected, this radio button specifies that Scanner 
HDR should not begin automatically at startup. 
− Area of Responsibility: The name of the AREA record 
representing the area of responsibility under which to issue HDR 
alarms. 
− Alarm Category: The name of the exception category for a 
component’s alarms. 
• File Maintenance 
− Rate at Which Recording File Is Flushed to Disk: The maximum 
time, in seconds, that may elapse before requesting the memory 
buffers to be flushed to disk. If this parameter is entered as zero, 
SCADA will not request the buffers to be flushed at a specific rate, 
although the operating system may still flush the buffers on a 
periodic basis depending on system loading. 
− Log Automatic File Deletion: When selected, this check box 
specifies that you want a message to be logged to the system 
activity log when HDR deletes an HDR file. This can occur if you 
choose the option to allow HDR to purge the oldest file to make 
room for a new one. 
− Purge Old Files to Make Room for New Files: When selected, 
this check box specifies that the oldest recording files can be 
deleted when the number of historical recording files approaches 
the maximum. This means that the newest recording files will 
always be available, and HDR will not shut down if there is no 
available disk space. 
− Log File Deletion by Operators: When selected, this check box 
specifies that you want a message to be logged to the system 
activity log when an operator deletes an HDR file via the HDR 
master display. 
2.4.4.10 SCADA API 
The SCADA API tab is used to configure the SCADA Applications 
Programming Interface (SCAPI) for the SCADA model. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
31
 
Note: Except for the SCAPI Initialize fields, all other fields on the SCADA 
API tab affect system performance. System performance tuning 
parameters should be entered or modified only by system analysts or 
programmers. 
• SCAPI Initialize 
− Do Not Assign SCADA Keys During Validation: When selected, 
this check box specifies that, during validation, no SCADA will be 
assigned to SUBSTN, DEVICE, SITE, RTU, ADRS, POINT, 
ANALOG, COUNT, CTRL, LIMIT, SETPNT, TFE, CHANEL, PATH, 
or RTU records. 
− Reset SCADA Keys: When selected, this check box specifies that 
new SCADA keys will be assigned that override any previously 
assigned keys. 
− Next SCADA Key Value: The value to be assigned to the next 
SCADA key. The default is for the first record to be assigned a key 
of 1, but this can be modified — for instance, to allow multiple sites 
to assign a unique range of numbers. 
− Number of History List Entries: The number of history list entries 
to maintain for each module. This information is used when 
debugging. 
• SCADA Server to Buffer Size (Bytes) 
− SCAPI Buffer Size: The maximum size, in bytes, of the buffer sent 
from SCSRV to the SCAPI user. This information is used for 
system performance tuning. 
− SCANNER Buffer Size: The maximum size, in bytes, of the buffer 
sent from SCSRV to the Scanner process. This information is used 
for system performance tuning. 
− CONTROL Buffer Size: The maximum size, in bytes, of the buffer 
sent from SCSRV to the Control process. This information is used 
for system performance tuning. 
• Handle Table 
− Initial Size: The initial number of handle table freelist entries. This 
information is used for system performance tuning. 
− Increment Size: The number of additional handle table freelist 
entries to allocate when the current list is full. 
− Maximum Size: The maximum number of handle table freelist 
entries to allocate. If zero, there is no limit in the size of the handle 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
32
 
table freelist. This information is used for system performance 
tuning. 
• Read Table 
− Initial Size: The initial number of read table freelist entries. This 
information is used for system performance tuning. 
− Increment Size: The number of additional read table freelist entries 
to allocate when the current list is full. 
− Maximum Size: The maximum number of read table freelist entries 
to allocate. If zero, there is no limit in the size of the read table 
freelist. This information is used for system performance tuning. 
• Limit Table 
− Initial Size: The initial number of limit table freelist entries. This 
information is used for system performance tuning. 
− Increment Size: The number of additional limit table freelist entries 
to allocate when the current list is full. 
− Maximum Size: The maximum number of limit table freelist entries 
to allocate. If zero, there is no limit in the size of the limit table 
freelist. This information is used for system performance tuning. 
2.4.4.11 Tagging/Notes 
The Tagging/Notes tab is used to specify the initial graphical parameters 
for the tagging and display notes functions of the SCADA subsystem. The 
initial state of the Intersite Tagging function is also specified here. 
Note: A display note is associated with a movable picture on an FG 
display. When a display note is created, the user enters the desired 
annotation, and then moves the associated picture to the desired display 
location. Optionally, the user can associate a display note with a tag 
group. 
The fields on the Tagging/Notes tab of the SCADA Parameters form are 
as follows: 
• General Information 
− Alarm Category: Alarm category for Tagging/Notes task. 
− Area of Responsibility: Permission area for Tagging/Notes task. 
− Fill Mark Display Timeout (Seconds): The timeout value (in 
seconds) for the Fill Mark display. A value of 0 denotes no timeout. 
If the user does not make entries before expiration of the timeout, 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
33
 
any tag creations or removals that were added during this particular 
tagging session will be canceled. 
− Annotate File Backup Timer: The timer value (in seconds) for the 
amount of time between transferring copies of the annotation files 
to the backup file server. The SCADA analyst uses the 
Tagnotes_Datafile utility to specify the backup file server. This 
specification is stored in the Tagnote.dat file. 
− Directory for Temporary Files: Path to the Tagging task’s 
temporary files on the local node. 
− Enable Tagging/Notes: A check mark specifies that the site-
specific Tagging task will be enabled at system startup. 
− Default Created By Fields to Console Name: A check mark 
specifies that the Tagging task will fill in the Created By fields with 
the user’s console name, as a default. 
− Default Created For Fields to Console Name: A check mark 
specifies that the Tagging task will fill in the Created For fields with 
the user’s console name, as a default. 
− Default Removed By Fields to Console Name: A check mark 
specifies that the Tagging task will fill in the Removed By fields with 
the user’s console name, as a default. 
− Default Removed For Fields to Console Name: A check mark 
specifies that the Tagging task will fill in the Removed For fields 
with the user’s console name, as a default. 
− Allow Annotate Files: A check mark specifies that the annotations 
entered on display notes will be captured. 
• Tags 
− Default Tag Type for Points: Designates the default tag type for 
status data points. 
− Default Tag Type for Analogs: Designates the default tag type for 
analog data points. 
− Default Alarm Location: Designates the default permission area 
for tags. 
− Sticky Tag Type: Designates the default sticky tag type. 
− Sticky Tag User ID: The user ID to use for all sticky tags. This is 
pre-filled upon creation. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
34
 
− Do Not Show Unlinked Tags: A check mark specifies that 
unlinked tags will not be included in the Tag Display lists. This 
option is only applicable for configurations that use Intersite 
Tagging. 
• Tag Groups 
− Default Area of Responsibility: Designates the tag group default 
permission area. 
− Default Alarm Location: Designates the tag group default alarm 
location. 
− Automatically Assign/De-assign Tag Groups: A check mark 
specifies that the Tagging Task will be enabled to assign and 
de-assign tag groups in the running system. 
− Automatically Add a Tag Group Record: A check mark specifies 
that the Tagging Task will be enabled to insert tag group records in 
online version of the SCADA database. 
− For Fields Required: A check mark specifies that the user must fill 
in the For fields. 
− User Identifier Field Required: A check mark specifies that the 
user must fill in the User Identifier fields. 
− Only Originating Site Can Modify Tags: A check mark specifies 
that only the originating site can modify, assign/de-assign, or 
remove Tag Groups. This option is only applicable for 
configurations that use Intersite Tagging. 
• Display Notes 
− Default Display Note Type: Designates the default display note 
type. 
− Sticky Display Note Type: Designates the default sticky note type. 
− Sticky Display Note User ID: The user ID to use for all sticky 
display notes. This is pre-filled upon creation. 
− Default Area of Responsibility: Designates the display notes 
default permission area. 
− Default Alarm Location: Designates the display notes default 
alarm location. 
Refer to the Tag Types and Display Notes reference tables for details 
about color, priority, and attribute options. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide

2.4.4.12 Control Defaults 
The Control Defaults tab is used to set default control actions in the 
SCADA model. 
• Controller Actions 
− Require Point To Be Selected Before Being Controlled: When 
selected, this check box specifies that the point must be selected 
before being controlled. 
− Allow Point To Be Selected Before Being Controlled: When 
selected, this check box specifies that the point may be controlled 
once it is selected. 
− Require Execute Step After Control Action: When selected, this 
check box specifies that the execute step must be completed 
before sending the control. 
− Allow Multiple Raise/Lower Without Reselecting: When 
selected, this check box allows multiple tap control actions (raise or 
lower) without re-selecting the point. If the execute step is required, 
then the multiple tap actions can only be in one direction, since the 
execute step is performed repeatedly (i.e., SELECT, RAISE, 
EXECUTE, EXECUTE, EXECUTE, CANCEL). 
2.4.4.13 Accumulator History 
The Accumulator History tab is used to model accumulator history 
information for the SCADA model. 
• Accumulator Information 
− Accumulator History Cold Start: When selected, this check box 
clears all accumulator history data points after a CPU cold start. 
− Fill Gaps in Accumulator Data: When selected, this check box 
specifies that missing accumulator history entries will be substituted 
with a placeholder. 
− Log Affected Meter Values During Delta Entry: When selected, 
this check box specifies logging of meter value changes that result 
from a changed delta value. 
2.4.4.14 Global Inhibit Definition 
The Global Inhibit Definition tab defines default SCADA alarm inhibit 
behavior for SCADA entities whose alarming the operator wants 
suppressed. These settings prevail unless the model builder has used the 
Override Inhibit Definition check box for the entity to define “custom” 
suppression behavior for the entity. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
36
 
• Logging: A check mark indicates that SCADA will issue neither an 
alarm nor an event. Therefore, entries will appear in neither the alarm 
list nor the event logs. 
• Unacknowledged Indication: A check mark indicates that SCADA will 
issue an event to the event log, but will not issue unacknowledged 
alarm indications to the alarm list. 
• Abnormal Count: The effect of this check box is to cause alarm 
messages for abnormals to have their date/time displayed in black in 
the Alarm displays instead of in red (assuming the HABITAT FG 
element set). 
• Abnormal Indication: Checking this box does not affect Alarm 
displays, but forces the record to appear Normal on SCADA displays. 
• Alarm Tone: A check mark indicates that notifications of alarm 
conditions via audible tones and horns will be suppressed. 
2.4.4.15 Specifying SCADA Model Parameters 
To set global parameters for the SCADA model, complete the following 
steps: 
1. Select the Model Paramete rs option in the View menu. 
The Model Parameters view displays. 
2. Expand the Model Parameters folder to expose its contents, and select 
the SCADA Parameters icon. 
3. Specify SCADA model  parameters on the SCADA Parameters form. 
4. Save the data. 
2.4.4.16 Specifying Site Permissions 
The default operating scheme for restricting intersite control and data 
access is to give all modeled SCADA sites access to all measurements. 
The procedures below explain how to restrict access using site 
permissions. 
A Site Permission specification determines how the SCADA system will 
restrict intersite “Read” and “Control” requests. The overall modeling 
process is as follows: 
• Enable intersite permission c hecking on a system-wide basis. 
• Create a list of grouped sites. 
• Place a reference on the status point, analog, pulse accumulator to the 
proper Site Permission group. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
37
 
• Restrict intersite control to a given controllable point or readable 
analog or count. 
To enable intersite permission checking on a system-wide basis, complete 
these steps: 
1. Navigate to the SCADA paramet ers area using the ModelerUI View 
menu, Model Parameters item. 
2. Expand the Model Parameters tree, and select the SCADA Parameters 
icon. 
3. From the Intersite Data tab, set the check box labeled “Enable 
Permissions Checking” ON. 
To create a Site Permission group, complete these steps: 
1. Navigate to the generic SCADA tables using the ModelerUI View 
menu. 
2. Right-click on the Site Permi ssions heading and select the Add 
SITPRM option. 
3. Enter a two-character Site Pe rmission group name, and save this 
instance of the SITPRM record. 
Notice that the Site Permission form contains a table for specifying the 
member sites of this Site Permission group. 
4. In the table on the Site Permission form, right-click in the area labeled 
“Site Name”. 
5. Select the Add a Row opti on in the pop-up menu that appears. 
6. Click in the entry box under the Site Name heading, and choose a site 
from the dropdown menu. 
Note: Site names are specified in the Sites Reference table. 
7. Add rows to specify other si tes for this Site Permission group. 
To specify a Site Permission group reference on a status point, analog, or 
pulse accumulator record, complete these steps: 
1. Navigate to the General Information tab of the status point, analog, or 
pulse accumulator record in question. 
2. Select the entry box labeled “Site Permissions”. 
3. Choose one of the site permissi on groups from the dropdown menu. 
To restrict access to the status point controls, complete these steps: 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
38
 
1. Navigate to the General Information tab of the status point of interest. 
2. Set the check box labeled “Allow Control from Foreign Sites” ON. 
2.4.4.17 Defining Alternate Limit Types 
Alternate limit types allow specification of multiple limit sets that are used 
at different times. For example, the alternate type “SEASON” could 
include the alternate limit sets “FALL”, “WINTER”, “SPRING”, and 
“SUMMER”. At any given time, exactly one of the child sets would be in 
effect for the alternate type. 
To define alternate limit types, complete the following steps: 
1. Select the Generic SCADA T ables option in the View menu. 
The Reference Tables view displays. 
2. Expand the Alternate Limit Types folder to expose its contents. 
a. To add a new alternate limit ty pe record, place the cursor on the 
Alternate Limit Types folder, click the right mouse button, and 
select the Add ALTTYP option. 
b. To update an existing alternate li mit type record, select its icon. 
3. Define alternate limit type informa tion using the Alternate Limit Type 
form. 
a. For each ALTTYP, add one or more ALTSET records. To add an 
ALTSET record, place the cursor on the ALTTYP icon, click the 
right mouse button, and select the Add ALTSET option. 
b. To update an existing alternate li mit set record, select its icon.

Display group records provide a means of calling up alternate displays 
when an entry is selected from one of the real-time exception displays. 
The default display is one identified with the same name as the ID field of 
the parent SUBSTN of the selected entry. If the DISP field is filled in and 
matches one of the entries in the DISP validation table, then the 
associated display is brought up instead of the default one-line display. 
To define display groups, complete the following steps:

The Reference Tables view displays.

2.4.6 SCADA Alarm Information 
The SCADA Alarm Information models alarm indications related to the 
monitored components of the Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition 
system. These definitions appear on the SCADA Alarm Information tab of 
a component or data point definition form. 
To model SCADA alarm information for a component or data point, enter 
details in the fields provided. 
• Alarm Location: Enter the text to appear on the Alarm location system 
line if an alarm is issued for equipment in the substation. Maximum 8 
characters. 
• Area of Responsibility: Enter the Permission name of the substation. 
Maximum 8 characters. 
• Initially Inhibit: A check mark indicates that, when the system first 
comes online, alarm indications for this SCADA entry will initially be 
inhibited. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
40
 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Database Mapping 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
41
• Override Default Inhibit Definition: A check mark indicates that the 
Operator Inhibit definition below overrides the Global Default Inhibit 
definition that appears on the SCADA Parameters form. 
• Operator Inhibit Definition: When the Override Default Inhibit 
Definition check box above is checked, the check boxes in this frame 
become active. The modeler uses them to specify which alarm 
indications below will be suppressed in the running SCADA system 
when the operator inhibits alarms pertinent to this SCADA entity. 
− Logging and Alarm List: A check mark indicates that SCADA will 
issue neither an alarm nor an event. Therefore, entries will appear 
in neither the alarm list nor the event logs. 
− Unacknowledged Alarm List: A check mark indicates that SCADA 
will issue an event to the event log, but will not issue 
unacknowledged alarm indications to the alarm list. 
− Abnormal for Alarm: The effect of this check box is to cause 
alarm messages for abnormals to have their date/time displayed in 
black in the Alarm displays instead of in red (assuming the 
HABITAT FG element set). 
− Abnormal for SCADA: Checking this box does not affect Alarm 
displays, but forces the record to appear Normal on SCADA 
displays. 
− Audible Tone: A check mark indicates that notifications of alarm 
conditions via audible tones and horns will be suppressed. 
• Permanently Inhibit Definition: The effect of the settings (checked 
boxes) in the Permanently Inhibit frame is to permanently suppress the 
corresponding alarm conditions regardless of whether the operator 
ever inhibits alarms for the SCADA entity during real-time operations 
(see the Operator Inhibit Definition check box explanations above for 
details about the conditions to which the Permanently Inhibit check 
boxes apply). 
 
 
3. Substation Topology 
This chapter provides information about the e-terrascada substation and 
monitored equipment topology (SUBTOP) and the procedures for defining 
it. The data structures that store monitored equipment measurements and 
the procedures for defining these structures are also described here. 
The SCADA calculations that read data from monitored equipment 
measurement structures, and write results back to the database, are 
described in another chapter. 
3.1 Defining Substations 
A substation is defined within an operating division, and its name must be 
globally unique. Information using substations is used by applications in all 
e-terraplatform subsystems: e-terrascada, e-terrageneration, 
e-terratransmission, and e-terrasimulator. 
To define a substation, complete the following steps: 
1. Click the Equipment  toolbar button. 
2. Navigate to the appropriate operati ng division icon for the substation, 
and expand it. 
a. To define a new substation, pl ace the cursor on the Operating 
Division icon, click the right mouse button, and select the Add 
Substation option. 
b. To update an existing substation, select its icon. 
3. Define substation information using the Substation form. 
4. Map the substation to the appr opriate SCADA measurements and 
controls.

EMS Schema Modeling Guide

the model builder can duplicate a substation in the power equipment tree

3.3 Defining Equipment Groups 
Power grid components that have the same Voltage Level requirements 
are assigned the same Equipment Group. 
Equipment Groups are part of the Power Equipment hierarchical (tree) 
structure. A group can consist of a set of components of the same type 
(such as grouped generators, grouped RTUs), a set of grouped 
components (as in the case of substations or companies), or a 
combination of both.

unique within a company. 
An “operating division” is defined as a grouping of substations under a 
common operating area. Operating division information is used by the 
e-terratransmission subsystem.

3.7.1.2 Specifying Operating Parameters 
The General Information tab of the Plant Controller form is used to model 
general plant controller operating parameters. 
To model Plant Controller parameters, enter data in the fields provided on 
the General Information tab as follows: 
• PLC Operation 
− Status: Dropdown list of default statuses for PLC operation. 
− Basepoint Mode: Dropdown list of default basepoint modes for 
PLC operation. 
− Participation Factor: This default value specifies the relative 
participation of the PLC in overall regulation duty. This must be a 
number greater than or equal to zero, typically 1.0. For example, if 
there are three PLCs currently participating in regulation, and you 
specify their regulation participation factors as 2, 1, and 1, 
respectively, then the first PLC performs 50% of the regulation duty 
and the other two each perform 25%. 
− Manual Participation Factor? When selected, this check box 
specifies that the value in the Participation Factor field will be used 
by the online participation factor calculation function. 
− Regulation Mode: Dropdown list of default regulation modes for 
PLC operation. 
− Regulation Preference: Dropdown list of default regulation 
preferences for the PLC. 
− Frequency Bias: The PLC regulating frequency bias in MW per 0.1 
Hertz; for example, 3.3% PLC capacity based on a 5% permanent 
droop characteristic. 
• Joint Ownership Information 
− Data Transfer Interface 
∗ Write to SCADA: When selected, this radio button specifies 
that JOU PLC information is output as data to SCADA. 
∗ Send to RTU: When selected, this radio button specifies that 
JOU PLC information is output as controls to a remote terminal 
unit. 
− JOU PLC: When selected, this check box specifies that there is a 
physical, jointly owned generating unit coupled with this plant 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Substation Topology 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
47
 
controller. Selecting this check box also enables updates to data on 
the Joint Ownership tab of the Generating Unit form. 
− Non-Physical PLC? When selected, this check box specifies that 
the unit is a non-physical, internally operated, jointly owned unit 
coupled with a plant controller. 
− Internal JOU PLC? When selected, this check box specifies that 
the unit is a physical, internally operated, jointly owned unit coupled 
with a plant controller. 
− Treat JOU? When selected, this check box specifies that the plant 
controller will honor MW requests from joint interest owners even 
when the physical unit is not operating in a joint unit mode. 
− Need AUT/MAN Telemetry? When selected, this check box 
specifies that the plant controller cannot send the auto/manual 
status to the JOU share owner. 
3.7.1.3 Specifying Control Parameters 
The Control Parameters tab of the Plant Controller form is used to model 
plant controller parameters. 
To model Plant Controller control parameters, enter data in the fields 
provided on the Control Parameters tab as follows: 
• Accept Control? When selected, this check box specifies that PLC 
can accept AGC control signals. 
• Setpoint Control? When selected, this check box specifies that MW 
setpoints are sent to the plant. Otherwise, MW raise/lower pulses are 
sent to the plant. 
• Raise/Lower SPFB Control? A check mark specifies that the PLC 
uses the SPFB value and bias terms in the PLC control error 
calculation instead of the actual MW. 
• Use Setpoint Filtering? A check mark specifies that PLC setpoints 
will be sent after filtering and processing by the PLC controller. 
• MW Settings 
− Pulse Calibration (MW/sec): The estimated pulse calibration for 
the PLC’s controller, in MW per second. This represents the MW 
value of a raise/lower pulse of one second’s duration. This value 
will be used to convert the required MW control for the PLC to a 
millisecond raise/lower pulse. This value must be greater than or 
equal to 0.0. This field applies only when pulse control commands 
are issued. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Substation Topology 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
48
 
− Setpoint Feedback Bias: Specify a value that represents the 
difference between the SPFB value and the actual MW. 
− Setpoint Deadband: The PLC setpoint signal deadband, in MW. 
The difference between the new setpoint MW control and the last 
setpoint sent must be equal to or greater than the setpoint 
deadband before it is issued. This field applies only when setpoint 
control commands are issued. Enabled when the Setpoint Control 
toggle is ON. 
− Pulse Discretization Factor: Specify an integer value. This field is 
not enterable when the Setpoint Control toggle is ON. 
• Pulse Length (Seconds) 
− Maximum: The maximum length of the SCADA control signal pulse 
to the generating plant PLC, in seconds. 
− Minimum: The minimum length of the SCADA control signal pulse 
to the generating plant PLC, in seconds. 
• PLC Response Times (secs) 
− Response Time Constant: The PLC response time constant for 
small changes, in seconds. A typical entry might be 20 seconds. 
− Min Time Between Forbidden Zone Crossing: The minimum 
number of seconds that a unit must remain on one side of a 
forbidden zone before being controlled by the other side. This 
parameter reduces oscillation across unit forbidden zones. 
• Boiler Energy Building Time (secs) 
− After Up Movement: The timer (in multiples of AGC cycles) 
representing the time needed by a PLC to build the boiler stored 
energy. 
− After Down Movement: The timer (in multiples of AGC cycles) 
representing the time needed by a PLC to build the boiler stored 
energy, in the down direction. 
3.8 Generating Units 
Generating units are substation devices. They are defined as single 
terminal devices and, therefore, they reside at only one node in the 
network and SCADA topologies. The name of the generating unit device 
can be 14 characters long, and it must be unique within a substation. 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Substation Topology 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
49
 
3.8.1.1 Steady-State Unit Model 
Unit input to the network is modeled as a direct contribution of power (W 
and R) at the node the unit is connected to, where W and R are the unit’s 
MW and MVAr outputs, respectively. 
Quantities for both W and R are often variables and have limits. The sign 
convention for both is positive for real and reactive power entering the 
network. 
If a unit does have associated unit VAr capability curves, a name of the 
selected unit VAr capability curve must be entered in the generating unit 
model. Each unit VAr capability curve is defined in a table, which shows a 
unit’s MVAr maximum and minimum limits for a given MW output. 
Units can also regulate voltage at any node in the same station. Such 
regulation is a standard time-dependent model applied to the voltage 
magnitude at the node defined as the unit’s regulation point in the 
generating unit model. A voltage regulation schedule can be assigned to 
the generating unit model if it does have one. 
If a unit is regulating voltage at a node, the solution process makes sure 
that the MVAr limit corresponding to that unit’s MW output is not violated. 
If the MVAr limit is violated, the MVAr output is reduced until the 
appropriate limit boundary is reached. Multiple unit regulation of a single 
node (ganged units) is also allowed. 
3.8.1.2 Dynamic Unit Model 
Although most of the static unit model used by the e-terratransmission 
and e-terrageneration applications is common to e-terrasimulator, two 
additions to the unit are required to model unit dynamic response in the 
e-terrasimulator environment: 
• A basic “swing equation” model of the torques and resulting speed of 
the unit’s rotating mass was added. 
• A response time was added to the unit voltage regulation model. 
The rotating mass represents the turbine/generator shaft and its inertia. 
The dynamics of the rotating mass are expressed as follows: 
2H dF/dt = PM – W – (D x deltaF) 
where: 
• H = The inertia constant in PU MW-sec 
• dF/dt = The time rate of frequency change in PU freq/sec 
• PM = The mechanical power of the turbine in PU MW 
• W = The electrical power output in PU 
• D = The damping coefficient in PU MW/PU freq 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Substation Topology 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
50
 
• deltaF = The frequency deviation from 60 (or 50) Hz in PU 
All units of an interconnected network are assumed to have the same 
speed and acceleration. Their common speed is the interconnection 
frequency, F (the island’s frequency). 
The synchronous generator is represented dynamically in the same way 
as in steady-state, except that a time constant is introduced: the time 
delay before unit switching from the automatic voltage regulation to 
manual voltage control. If a voltage regulating unit exceeds its limit for this 
amount of time, it becomes a non-regulating unit with reactive power fixed 
at a limit. Similarly, a non-regulating unit begins regulating again if the 
voltage comes back in range for the specified number of seconds. 
3.8.1.3 Incremental Heat Rate Curves 
Incremental heat rate curves (IHRCs) are modeled for each unit whose 
generation type defines it as economically dispatchable. You construct the 
curve by entering a series of points that give the incremental fuel units 
(Mega BTU) per MWH at the MW level specified. AGC interpolates the 
points linearly to determine the incremental heat rate at intermediate 
points. 
Incremental heat rate curves must increase monotonically between the 
minimum economic dispatch point and the unit capability. An IHRC is 
never extrapolated, and it must be defined over the complete range of 
generation. The first point of the curve must occur at zero MWs. 
Each IHRC corresponds to a PLANT and UNIT identifier. The unit’s 
incremental heat rate curve is constructed as a series of points, each of 
which specifies the incremental heat in MBTU per MWH required to 
maintain generation at a specified MW level. 
3.8.1.4 Forbidden Zones 
Generating units with certain physical characteristics have a region of their 
MW output that is not desirable for operation. For hydroelectric units, it 
may be a region of excessive vibration due to cavitation. For thermal units, 
it may be a region of uneconomical operation due to steam turbine valve 
point loading. These regions are known as “forbidden zones”, “prohibited 
operating zones”, “gray zones”, or “disallowed operating ranges”. All of 
these will be referred to as “forbidden zones” in this document. 
Forbidden zones are defined by their upper and lower bounds. These 
limits are given in gross or net MW, consistent with the other MW values 
for the unit. 
Each Forbidden Zone record represents a separate forbidden zone. Units 
without telemetered forbidden zone limits may contain multiple Forbidden 
Zone records. Units with telemetered forbidden zone limits can only 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Substation Topology 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
51
 
contain one Forbidden Zone record, a restriction enforced during 
GENMODEL validation. 
When multiple Forbidden Zone records exist, their limits must be in 
ascending order. Forbidden Zone limits are not confined by unit economic 
or LFC limits, but are confined by the unit capacity limit. 
Two values may be checked against the forbidden zone limits: unit 
basepoints and unit desired generation. If flags have been set to enforce 
the forbidden zone limits in the Economic Dispatch (ED), unit basepoints 
are calculated normally and then compared to the zone limits. If unit 
basepoints are found within the forbidden zone, they are adjusted to one 
of the zone limits based upon the current generation, the calculated 
unrestricted limit, and the basepoint zone crossing percentage. The 
basepoint zone crossing percentage is the amount of the zone that must 
be crossed by the basepoint before the adjusted basepoint will be set to 
the zone limit that is opposite the current generation. 
The desired generation for each Plant Controller (PLC) is the PLC’s 
basepoint plus the PLC’s regulation component. The desired generation 
for a PLC with the forbidden zone enforcement flag set is checked against 
the forbidden zones: 
• The PLC is allowed to contro l the unit normally if the desired 
generation is not in or across a forbidden zone. 
• The PLC is allowed to control t he unit across the zone if the desired 
generation crosses the forbidden zone, does not violate the LFC limit, 
and the time between crossing is greater than the minimum time 
setting. 
• The PLC control is limited to the forbidden zone limit closest to the 
current generation if: 
− The desired generation is in the forbidden zone. 
− The desired generation is acro ss the zone without the minimum 
time between crossing having expired. 
− The current generation is too far away from the forbidden zone 
limit. 
The processing for desired generation related to forbidden zones is 
effective only for those PLCs that have the flag to enforce forbidden zone 
restrictions in unit controls set. 
An alarm is issued if the PLC is controlling a unit across a forbidden zone, 
and the AGC or the PLC is suspended. An alarm is also issued when the 
PLC is controlling a unit across a forbidden zone, the AGC or PLC is 
Proprietary – See Copyright Page  Substation Topology 
EMS Schema Modeling Guide 
52
 
paused, and the time delay for the AGC or PLC to wait before entering the 
“Suspend” state is set to prevent the suspend state.
